CHAPTER - 11
The Standing Instructions given for Preparedness about flood, 
cyclone to department / Zones
[At emergent time when there is a forecasting (about - flood / cyclone)]
The Standing Instructions given by Commissioner for starting the activities regarding possibility of cyclone / rain. 
Rescue Operations : 
1.  Garden Departments should keep the equipment, tracto rs ready along with the staff for cutting the trees. 
2.  Check up whether all the vehicles / equipments of fire brigade are kept in working condition or not. 
3.  Contact the NGOs; ask them to keep the volunteers ready. 
4.  The keys of relief centres / schools should be obtained. Be ready in advance for the transfer of the people of the 
affected areas keeping in mind heavy rains & tidal waves. Arrange for vehicles for publishing messages in all zones.  
5.  The Town Planner and all the Zonal Officer should inform th e concerned agencies for removing all the hoard-
ings put on the roads. 
6.  All the Zonal Officers should take precautionary measures b y keeping necessary staff ready for continuous 
discharge (disposal) of water from the low lying areas likely to have accumulation of water due to nearby creeks 
having inflow of water due to tides. 
7.  When forecast of severe cyclone and heavy rain is mad e, all Zonal Officers should take necessary steps so 
that because of such calamities deaths / accidents do not take place. 
8.  All Zonal Officers should arrange for keeping generators in wo rking condition at important places. 
9.  In every meeting held under their command / control they should go on reminding about the disaster manage-
ment plan prepared regarding flood & cyclone. 
Relief Operations : 
10.  All zones control room and central control room should be continued till the receipt of further instructions.  
11.  No officer / employee should leave the head quarters. 
12.  Contingency plan should be kept hardly and relief operations should be carried out accordingly.   
13.  The staff, equipments etc should be kept ready for relief work in every zone.  
14.  Day Off’s, Holiday (leave) of all Fire Brigade staff should be cancelled and they should be given alert message. 
15.  Whenever there is a possibility of cyclone to blow at a speed of 100 km per hour & this speed is likely to go up 
to 150 km per hour, serious preparations should be made. (for rescue & other activities )
16.  Whenever there is a possibility of heavy rains preparations should be so made that rescue & relief operations 
can be started immediately. 
17.  Alert message should be given to the staff so that all of them can be present at short notice. 

[page 90]
DISASTER MANAGEMENT  AND PREPAREDNESS PLANqq q q
 84))
18.  Whenever the cyclone forecast is continuing all Zonal Officers should continue their activities of precautionary 
measures in the concerned areas entrusted to them. 
19.  Arrangement should be made to maintain the message properly in the Log book at the central control room & 
zone control rooms during the cyclone / flood / natural calamity. 
20.  Sometimes difficulties in the areas in the city due to some matters in the areas adjoining the city. All the Zonal Of-
ficers should take these into account and should take necessary steps through the nearby zone regarding them. 
Aimed at Health : 
21.  For treatment during relief work, hospital, medicine etc and also the emergency staff of the hospital should be 
kept ready.  
22.  The medical van of the Surat Municipal Corporation should be kept ready. The concerned doctors should be 
kept in touch and should be told to remain present on (short notice) information. 
23.  For additional patients necessary faculties should be kept ready in the municipal hospitals & health centres.
24.  Immediate after the water recedes the arrangement for cleaning the mud & dirt form the low lying slum areas 
due to accumulation of water during tides should be made by all Zonal Officers.  
Enlightenment of People :
25.  In concerned zones, all Zonal Officers should inform conc erned Municipal Councilors and office bearers of 
NGOs regarding the concerned emergency (which has occurred) 
26.  In order that the public comes to know regarding precautionary measures to be taken at the time of cyclone, 
the PRO should publish the matter through the newspapers and local TV channel. 
Precautionary Measures : 
27.  At the time of cyclone, The Garden department officers should keep in contact with contractors who are given 
the contract of cutting the trees & inform them to keep their men ready. 
28.  Deputy Eng. / Ex. Eng. (street light) and all Zonal Offic ers should take steps so that there is no loss / loss of 
public life due to the light poles.
29.  Brackets of the high mast towers in the city should be taken down as precautionary measures.
30.  The Garden Superintendent should take into account the forecast of cyclone and should arrange for a survey 
of old, likely to fall down and risky trees and take precautionary measures. 

[page 91]
DISASTER MANAGEMENT  AND PREPAREDNESS PLANqq q q
 85))
 
CHAPTER - 12
Flood in the Creeks: Information & Management
 Mainly Varachha creeks, Koyali creeks, Mithi creeks, Kankra creek, Bhedvad creek, Chhaprabhatha creek etc 
pass through the Surat city. Their discharge to sea is through main creek and Mindhoela river. The water of Tena 
creek flows directly into the sea. In the upper flow there a re taluka areas of Bardoli, Kamrej, Mahuwa (part) and 
Palsana. 
 The catchments area / length of the various creeks passing through Surat city is as under.  
Sr.No. Name of the creek Catchment area (sq.km) Length (km) Creek Length (km)
1 Koyali Creek 13.25 8.35 8.35
2 Mithi Creek 154.05 53.28 6.50
3 Kankra Creek 226.42 8.15 8.15
4 Bhedwad Creek 40.50 15.00 11.01
5 Varachha Creek 85.54 24.57 5.07
6 Chhaprabhatha Creek 26.86 7.50 6.205
7 Tena Creek 25.91 6.84 - 
8 Sonari Creek 71.77 21.28 7.94
 Due to (i) heavy rain in the catchments areas of these cre eks flow of water increases (ii) local rain / flood in 
the parent river etc as also (iii) there is lack of disposal of water in the creeks due to the effect of tides in the sea, 
the water in the creeks over flow in the following low lying areas and areas of societies around as listed below are 
generally affected.
 East Zone (varachha) - Areas on the outskirts of Nana Varachha, Kapodra, Karanj. 
 South East Zone ( Limbayat) - Magob, Dumbhal, Kamrunagar, Mithikhadi, Ratanjinagar, Krantinagar, Raonagar, 
Sanjaynagar, Govindnagar, Salimnagar, Mandarwaje low cost colony, 181/184 Bhathena and low lying area of 
Anjana Chimni Tekra.
 South Zone (Udhana) - Sanjaynagar, premnagar.
 South West (Athwa) - Rasulabad, Azadnagar, Santoshinaga r, Indiranagar.
 North Zone (Katargam) - Amroli, Ganeshpura- Kosad - GHB colony, Kosad village area, motavarachha.
 In addition to this, if heavy rain falls in the city area during this period, care should be taken that enough water 
does not flow out through the storm water drainage line laid down by Surat Municipal Corporation and as such in 
some low lying area of the city, there may be water accumulation or increase in the level of the accumulated water.
Due to high tide waves, sea water enters these creeks whereas generally creeks direct by dispose off the water into the sea. 
 This Disaster Management Preparedness Plan includes most of the preplanning and necessary steps to be 
taken during the disasters situation that arises due to hea vy rain, flood, cyclone in the areas in the banks of the 
above creeks in the city for seeing that there should not be loss of life & property of the people or it should be the 
least and after that for carrying out rescue & relief operations for establishing the normal condition and there after 
doing necessary activities of rehabilitation. 
	 All the areas around khadi and low line areas were affected by heavy rains in Surat and District in 1998 July. After 
that during beginning of Aug-2013 & end of June-2005 due to heavy rains in catchments areas in the districts. 
 In order that the information about upper catchments areas & of rains is continuously available & is continu-
ously monitored, it should be provided in the form attached herewith to the central control & all zonal controls. 

[page 92]
DISASTER MANAGEMENT  AND PREPAREDNESS PLANqq q q
 86))
For the purpose the concerned persons should remain in contact with Taluka / district control rooms of the 
collectorate.  
  For monitoring of the level of water & is continuously between Bhathena, Jivanjyot & South Zone Office and under 
the bridge on Mithi Creek & for the readings of the level under the bridge near Limbayat (Mithi Creek), Bhedwad 
creek, kankra creek & koyali creek near the south zone office and on the bridges on other creeks marking / paint-
ing taking into account GTS, every two hours reading should be taken and filling the details of water levels of all 
the creeks in the attached form it should be submitted to the central control for their information.
 The concerned zone should arrange for proper information of floods in the creeks to the public so that people 
can shift to safe places in appropriate time.

CHAPTER - 13
TAPI Flood Control Scheme 
 
Under the “TAPI River Flood Control Scheme”, Surat Manicipal Corporation has Con-
struct The Flood Control Stucture as below in surat City.
1. To prevent the flood in the lower level  areas of Varachha, Flood control structure 
(Sluics Regulator) has been built on Varachha Khadi.
2. To Prevent the flood in the lower level areas of Chhaprabhatha, the flood control 
structure (Sluice Regulator), has been built On Chhaprabhatha Khadi.

(200)
At the time of Disaster: Do this necessary 
Earthquake 
 
 Earthquake is a type of natural disaster. Earthquakes occur due to  abnormal 
movements in the upper layers of the earth. Generally: Any disa ster is forecasted and 
advance preparations are made to prevent the disaster and reduce the loss of life due 
to the disaster to a great extent. Whereas in the case of ea rthquake, it is not possible to 
predict. Due to which advance preparation cannot be done. T he immediate aftermath of 
an earthquake is a very delicate time. The operations related  to the disasters caused by 
the earthquake are very difficult. First of all rescue the victims  / trapped people. It is 
necessary to carry out the work of providing first aid to the inju red and taking the 
affected to a safe place if necessary, to the hospital. It is necessary to raise a relief and 
rescue team on a permanent basis in an emergency like an earthqu ake. So in case of 
storm we have made advance preparations like rescue and relief te ams, heavy 
machinery, essential items etc. It is very important to keep it handy. In the case of 
earthquakes, the second and third post-disaster phases play a ve ry important role. In 
the case of an earthquake, the first phase focuses on immediat e treatment of the 
affected, disposal of dead bodies, prevention of the spread  of epidemics, etc. While in 
the second phase, it is necessary to provide facilities like foo d grains, water and shelter 
to the affected people in coordination with all governmen t departments and social 
voluntary organizations. In the third phase, permanent resettle ment works and provision 
of cash assistance are to be done for the affected. The magn itude of the earthquake 
can be known from the Richter scale. The Richter scale can be u sed to determine the 
effect of the damages on the Richter scale score. In addition  to the effect of damage on 
the Richter scale, an idea of the damage caused by an earthq uake on the Richter scale 
is obtained from the scale mentioned below. 
 
 Apart from this, the Do's & Don'ts for people to do at th e time of earthquake are 
as per the attached picture.

(201)

generators etc handly
ready.

District and local crises group is included in the Chemical Accident Rules-1996. these
objectives of these groups are is under:
Unfortunate occurrences due to the calamity can be avoided, the same 
Happenings in the industrial organizations can also be avoided if planning
should be done accordingly to minimise casualties.
To Provide necessary warning and information about calamity(or Disaster)
and guidance regarding the calamity, publicity/transmission should be done.

 1 ))
DISASTER MANAGEMENT  AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN
Flood
Do not enter in unknown logged flood water. Buildings to be built with higher plinth in the 
area near to River, water streams & sea.
Learn to swim and rescue the one drowning.
Follow officials’ instructions 
regarding relocating to safe 
place with highest priority.
Move the pets to safe location. Every family member should always carry identity card. 
Shift to safe and secure place.
Keep emergency kit, eatables 
and drinkables handy.
Drink chlorinated 
water.
Drink boiled water, 
eat fresh food.
Before Flood
• Don’t spread rumors. Keep Calm. Don’t be afraid.
• Keep your mobile phone charged.
• Know the safest way to nearest safe place.
• To know the latest updates about weather listen to the rad io or watch 
television.
• Be in touch with local authorities and follow their instructions.
• Keep rope, lantern, flashlight, extra batteries handy.
• Keep ready first aid kit along with extra medicines for snake  bites and 
diarrhea and vomiting.
• Keep umbrella with you. To prevent snake bites or other poisonous  
creatures, keep a wooden stick.
• To save animals do not tie them up.
• Make available clean water, dry food items, candles, match b oxes, kero-
sene. 
While Shifting
• Keep clothes, necessary medicines, valuable items,  personal documents 
in waterproof packing 
• Keep furniture & other home tools on table or bed.
• To prevent flood waters entering the house through drainage , put sand 
bags on top of it to keep them closed.
• Turn off the lights and gas cylinder before leaving the hous e.
• Keep the house locked and reach the safe place as direct ed.
• Move to the higher places where people and animals can take shelter
• Avoid entering into deep and unknown waters.
During the flood
• Avoid entering into the flood waters. If necessary wear proper  shoes 
before entering.
• Stay away from drainages and ways of water disposal path s.
• Stay away from electric poles and electric wires lying on grou nds to 
avoid electric shocks.
• Do not keep the children hungry.
• To sterilize the surroundings, use lime or bleaching powder.
• Eat fresh cooked or dry food. Keep the food in closed vessels.
• Drink boiled water or sterilized water as per instructions of He alth 
Department.
After Flood
• Return home only after the instructions from local authorities.
• Do not let children go into flood water or nearby flood water.
• Avoid using damaged electrical equipment. Use them  only after getting it 
verified from electrician.
• Be careful with broken electric poles, damaged bridges, wate r streams, 
debris, broken glass or other sharp objects.
• Follow the advice of workers associated with  local authorities and 
emergency services.
• Inform on specified emergency control room phone numbers for r elief 
and rescue operations in emergency.
• Do not eat food affected by flood water.
• To prevent malaria, use mosquito net.
Do this invariably at the time of Disaster

[page 211]
 2 ))
DISASTER MANAGEMENT  AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN
Keep essential items gathered and handy.
Thunderstorms 
/Cyclone
Take the injured to hospital immediately.
Strengthen the 
doors and windows 
of house.
Listen & follow the news given from 
government medium and officials.
Close 
all the 
doors 
and win-
dows of 
house.
Fishermen should avoid going into the sea and park 
the boats at safe place.
To save animals do not tie them up and move them 
to safe place.
Avoid touching the cables.
Before Thundestorms /
Cyclone
• Do not spread rumors, keep calm, do not be 
afraid.
• Keep your mobile charged.
• Listen to news and warnings regularly.
• It is advisable to use battery operated radio 
set.
• Verify that the radio set is in operational 
condition.
• Try to stay in touch with local officials.
• It is advisable to relocate immediately after 
first warning if you live in hazardous/risky 
area.
• Adhere to instruction for relocation from 
government if any.
During Thunderstorms /
Cyclone
• Avoid taking shelter in decrepit house or 
under a tree.
• Listen to the news on radio or TV and follow 
the instructions given.
• Do not take risk of going outside during 
thunderstorm/cyclone.
• If no instruction is received for evacuating 
the house, take shelter in the strongest part 
of house and stay inside.
• It is not advisable to travel through train or 
go for sailing during thunderstorm/cyclone.
• Avoid staying on the terrace of high-rise 
buildings or houses.
• If outside the house, stay away from electric 
wires, electric poles or electric equipment.
After Thunderstorms/
Cyclone
• Do not try to enter damaged buildings.
• Help the affected people.
• Use phones only for necessary purposes.
• Stay in the house only if the house is safe.
• Go outside the house only after confirming 
that the thunderstorm is over.
• Follow the instructions of local officials.
• Wait for the directive regarding safety on 
radio and televisions.
• Stay away from broken electric cables, 
bridges and buildings.
• Be cautious of metal plates, pieces of glass, 
snake and other poisonous creatures while 
passing through debris.
• Stay away from flooded area.
• Wait for 24 hours before leaving for sailing 
or for fishing.
Do this invariably at the time of Disaster
